What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I came in because... lately at work, I've had to be a lot more out in front of people. It's really bothering me, even though everyone else tells me they barely see anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Is it only your appearance that's bothering you about this uneven-looking part of your face, or are you also worried about other things, like its potential impact on your health?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just my appearance. I'm not worried about my health or anything like that. Um, I recently missed part of a planning deadline because I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. It just kind of took over. I haven't exactly kept track of whether it's there every single minute or if it comes and goes. I just know it's on my mind enough to slow me down at work, like I said, but... yeah, I couldn't really tell you the exact pattern of it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about this one uneven-looking part of your face, or do you also worry about other aspects of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just this one thing. I don't really worry about anything else when it comes to how I look. It's just that one uneven part of my face that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about the uneven part of your face is causing you?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I don't know if I'd say I'm in distress, exactly. I mean, it's frustrating and it definitely gets in the way of my work, like I mentioned with the event materials. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of how much distress it causes. It just... bothers me a lot when I have to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would see this uneven part of your face as a significant problem if they looked closely at your photos?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... no. Whenever I bring it up, everyone else says they barely see anything at all. But I just can't stop staring at it in photos. That's actually why I came in to see you. Ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just fixate on this one uneven-looking part of my face, even if other people tell me it's not a big deal to them.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven part of your face caused you to avoid social interactions or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I've never really timed it or anything, but... it's mostly just when I'm working with photos or videos for an event. I might get stuck looking at it then, but I wouldn't say it adds up to a whole hour every day. Once I step away from the pictures, I don't really sit around thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about the uneven part of your face happen to you every day?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not every day. Like I said, it really only comes up when I have to review media for my job. If I'm just going about a normal day where I don't have to look at pictures of myself, I don't even really think about it. It's very tied to when I actually see myself on screen.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I came in because... lately at work, I've had to be a lot more out in front of people. I plan events, so I end up in photos a lot more now. And ever since that started, I just can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in the pictures. It's really bothering me, even though everyone else tells me they barely see anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Is it only your appearance that's bothering you about this uneven-looking part of your face, or are you also worried about other things, like its potential impact on your health?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just my appearance. I'm not worried about my health or anything like that. Um, I recently missed part of a planning deadline because I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. It just kind of took over. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I came in because... lately at work, I've had to be a lot more out in front of people. I plan events, so I end up in photos a lot more now. And ever since that started, I just can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in the pictures. It's really bothering me, even though everyone else tells me they barely see anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Is it only your appearance that's bothering you about this uneven-looking part of your face, or are you also worried about other things, like its potential impact on your health?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just my appearance. I'm not worried about my health or anything like that. I know it's not a medical problem... it's strictly just how uneven it looks to me, especially when I have to look at those photos.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven-looking part of your face been affecting your daily life or work in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has. Um, I recently missed part of a planning deadline because I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. It just kind of took over. And now... well, even just regular things like vendor calls and meeting prep take me a lot longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Is this concern about the uneven part of your face something that you think about all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of whether it's there every single minute or if it comes and goes. I just know it's on my mind enough to slow me down at work, like I said, but... yeah, I couldn't really tell you the exact pattern of it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about this one uneven-looking part of your face, or do you also worry about other aspects of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just this one thing. I don't really worry about anything else when it comes to how I look. It's just that one uneven part of my face that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about the uneven part of your face is causing you?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I don't know if I'd say I'm in distress, exactly. I mean, it's frustrating and it definitely gets in the way of my work, like I mentioned with the event materials. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of how much distress it causes. It just... bothers me a lot when I have to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would see this uneven part of your face as a significant problem if they looked closely at your photos?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... no. Whenever I bring it up, everyone else says they barely see anything at all. But I just can't stop staring at it in photos. That's actually why I came in to see you. Ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just fixate on this one uneven-looking part of my face, even if other people tell me it's not a big deal to them.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven part of your face caused you to avoid social interactions or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it doesn't stop me from hanging out with my friends or going out on the weekends. It's really just when I have to look at myself in pictures or videos for work that it becomes an issue for me. Outside of that, I don't really avoid social things.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend more than an hour a day thinking about the uneven part of your face?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think it's that much. I've never really timed it or anything, but... it's mostly just when I'm working with photos or videos for an event. I might get stuck looking at it then, but I wouldn't say it adds up to a whole hour every day. Once I step away from the pictures, I don't really sit around thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about the uneven part of your face happen to you every day?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not every day. Like I said, it really only comes up when I have to review media for my job. If I'm just going about a normal day where I don't have to look at pictures of myself, I don't even really think about it. It's very tied to when I actually see myself on screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of the uneven part of your face is accurate, even when others tell you they don't see it as a problem?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I don't think I've ever pointed it out to anyone else, so no one has actually told me they don't see it. I just know what I see when I'm sorting through the event photos. I guess I don't really know if my perception is completely accurate or not... it's just what stands out to me when I look at the screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the uneven part of your face is actually uneven, regardless of what others say?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do think it's actually uneven. Like I said, I haven't really asked anyone else what they think, but when I look at the photos or the videos from an event, I can see it. So... yeah, I believe what I'm seeing is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face might be exaggerated or not entirely accurate, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm not totally sure. I mean, I see it when I look at the pictures, but I don't really know if I'm making it a bigger deal in my head than it actually is. It's hard to say if it's exaggerated or not since I haven't really talked to anyone else about it. I don't think I've ever pointed it out to anyone else, so no one has actually told me they don't see it. I just know what I see when I'm sorting through the event photos. I guess I don't really know if my perception is completely accurate or not... it's just what stands out to me when I look at the screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the uneven part of your face is actually uneven, regardless of what others say?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do think it's actually uneven. Like I said, I haven't really asked anyone else what they think, but when I look at the photos or the videos from an event, I can see it. So... yeah, I believe what I'm seeing is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face might be exaggerated or not entirely accurate, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm not totally sure. It's hard to say if it's exaggerated or not since I haven't really talked to anyone else about it. I just know it bothers me when I'm looking at the event media.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the unevenness you see in your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I guess there is a possibility. I mean, I know I said a minute ago that I hadn't really asked anyone, but... the whole reason I came in to see you is because ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just can’t stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in all the event photos. \n\nWhen I finally did break down and mention it to a couple of people recently, they told me they barely see anything at all. And now... well, even just regular things like vendor calls and meeting prep take me a lot longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Is this concern about the uneven part of your face something that you think about all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of whether it's there every single minute or if it comes and goes. I just know it's on my mind enough to slow me down at work, like I said, but... yeah, I couldn't really tell you the exact pattern of it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about this one uneven-looking part of your face, or do you also worry about other aspects of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just this one thing. I don't really worry about anything else when it comes to how I look. It's just that one uneven part of my face that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about the uneven part of your face is causing you?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I don't know if I'd say I'm in distress, exactly. I mean, it's frustrating and it definitely gets in the way of my work, like I mentioned with the event materials. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of how much distress it causes. It just... bothers me a lot when I have to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would see this uneven part of your face as a significant problem if they looked closely at your photos?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... no. Whenever I bring it up, everyone else says they barely see anything at all. But I just can't stop staring at it in photos. That's actually why I came in to see you. Ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just fixate on this one uneven-looking part of my face, even if other people tell me it's not a big deal to them.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven part of your face caused you to avoid social interactions or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it doesn't stop me from hanging out with my friends or going out on the weekends. It's really just when I have to look at myself in pictures or videos for work that it becomes an issue for me. Outside of that, I don't really avoid social things.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend more than an hour a day thinking about the uneven part of your face?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think it's that much. I've never really timed it or anything, but... it's mostly just when I'm working with photos or videos for an event. I might get stuck looking at it then, but I wouldn't say it adds up to a whole hour every day. Once I step away from the pictures, I don't really sit around thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about the uneven part of your face happen to you every day?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not every day. Like I said, it really only comes up when I have to review media for my job. If I'm just going about a normal day where I don't have to look at pictures of myself, I don't even really think about it. It's very tied to when I actually see myself on screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of the uneven part of your face is accurate, even when others tell you they don't see it as a problem?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I don't think I've ever pointed it out to anyone else, so no one has actually told me they don't see it. I just know what I see when I'm sorting through the event photos. I guess I don't really know if my perception is completely accurate or not... it's just what stands out to me when I look at the screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the uneven part of your face is actually uneven, regardless of what others say?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do think it's actually uneven. Like I said, I haven't really asked anyone else what they think, but when I look at the photos or the videos from an event, I can see it. So... yeah, I believe what I'm seeing is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face might be exaggerated or not entirely accurate, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm not totally sure. I mean, I see it when I look at the pictures, but I don't really know if I'm making it a bigger deal in my head than it actually is. It's hard to say if it's exaggerated or not since I haven't really talked to anyone else about it. I just know it bothers me when I'm looking at the event media.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the unevenness you see in your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I guess there is a possibility. I mean, I know I said a minute ago that I hadn't really asked anyone, but... the whole reason I came in to see you is because ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just can’t stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in all the event photos. \n\nWhen I finally did break down and mention it to a couple of people recently, they told me they barely see anything at all. So... maybe it isn't actually there the way I see it, or maybe it's just really minor. But even knowing they don't see it, I still can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself trying to stop thinking about the uneven part of your face, but you can't?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't think I've actually tried to stop thinking about it. Like, I don't sit there and try to force it out of my head. It just sort of... happens when I'm looking at the photos from work or getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say I try to stop and can't, I just kind of focus on it when it comes up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

So... yeah, I believe what I'm seeing is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face might be exaggerated or not entirely accurate, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm not totally sure. I mean, I see it when I look at the pictures, but I don't really know if I'm making it a bigger deal in my head than it actually is. It's hard to say if it's exaggerated or not since I haven't really talked to anyone else about it. I just know it bothers me when I'm looking at the event media.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the unevenness you see in your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I guess there is a possibility. I mean, I know I said a minute ago that I hadn't really asked anyone, but... the whole reason I came in to see you is because ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just can’t stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in all the event photos. \n\nWhen I finally did break down and mention it to a couple of people recently, they told me they barely see anything at all. So... maybe it isn't actually there the way I see it, or maybe it's just really minor. But even knowing they don't see it, I still can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself trying to stop thinking about the uneven part of your face, but you can't?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't think I've actually tried to stop thinking about it. Like, I don't sit there and try to force it out of my head. It just sort of... happens when I'm looking at the photos from work or getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say I try to stop and can't, I just kind of focus on it when it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that thoughts about the uneven part of your face intrude into your mind when you're not intentionally thinking about it?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It doesn't just pop up out of nowhere. I mean... if I'm just going about my day or working on a schedule for an event, I'm not really thinking about it. It's pretty much only when I'm confronted with a picture of myself from work, or when I'm looking in the mirror getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say it intrudes when I'm doing other things. It's just tied to when I'm actually looking at my face.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I came in because... lately at work, I've had to be a lot more out in front of people. I plan events, so I end up in photos a lot more now. And ever since that started, I just can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in the pictures. It's really bothering me, even though everyone else tells me they barely see anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Is it only your appearance that's bothering you about this uneven-looking part of your face, or are you also worried about other things, like its potential impact on your health?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just my appearance. I'm not worried about my health or anything like that. I know it's not a medical problem... it's strictly just how uneven it looks to me, especially when I have to look at those photos.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven-looking part of your face been affecting your daily life or work in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has. Um, I recently missed part of a planning deadline because I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. It just kind of took over. And now... well, even just regular things like vendor calls and meeting prep take me a lot longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Is this concern about the uneven part of your face something that you think about all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of whether it's there every single minute or if it comes and goes. I just know it's on my mind enough to slow me down at work, like I said, but... yeah, I couldn't really tell you the exact pattern of it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about this one uneven-looking part of your face, or do you also worry about other aspects of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just this one thing. I don't really worry about anything else when it comes to how I look. It's just that one uneven part of my face that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about the uneven part of your face is causing you?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I don't know if I'd say I'm in distress, exactly. I mean, it's frustrating and it definitely gets in the way of my work, like I mentioned with the event materials. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of how much distress it causes. It just... bothers me a lot when I have to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would see this uneven part of your face as a significant problem if they looked closely at your photos?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... no. Whenever I bring it up, everyone else says they barely see anything at all. But I just can't stop staring at it in photos. That's actually why I came in to see you. Ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just fixate on this one uneven-looking part of my face, even if other people tell me it's not a big deal to them.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven part of your face caused you to avoid social interactions or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it doesn't stop me from hanging out with my friends or going out on the weekends. It's really just when I have to look at myself in pictures or videos for work that it becomes an issue for me. Outside of that, I don't really avoid social things.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend more than an hour a day thinking about the uneven part of your face?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think it's that much. I've never really timed it or anything, but... it's mostly just when I'm working with photos or videos for an event. I might get stuck looking at it then, but I wouldn't say it adds up to a whole hour every day. Once I step away from the pictures, I don't really sit around thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about the uneven part of your face happen to you every day?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not every day. Like I said, it really only comes up when I have to review media for my job. If I'm just going about a normal day where I don't have to look at pictures of myself, I don't even really think about it. It's very tied to when I actually see myself on screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of the uneven part of your face is accurate, even when others tell you they don't see it as a problem?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I don't think I've ever pointed it out to anyone else, so no one has actually told me they don't see it. I just know what I see when I'm sorting through the event photos. I guess I don't really know if my perception is completely accurate or not... it's just what stands out to me when I look at the screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the uneven part of your face is actually uneven, regardless of what others say?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do think it's actually uneven. Like I said, I haven't really asked anyone else what they think, but when I look at the photos or the videos from an event, I can see it. So... yeah, I believe what I'm seeing is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face might be exaggerated or not entirely accurate, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm not totally sure. I mean, I see it when I look at the pictures, but I don't really know if I'm making it a bigger deal in my head than it actually is. It's hard to say if it's exaggerated or not since I haven't really talked to anyone else about it. I just know it bothers me when I'm looking at the event media.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the unevenness you see in your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I guess there is a possibility. I mean, I know I said a minute ago that I hadn't really asked anyone, but... the whole reason I came in to see you is because ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just can’t stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in all the event photos. \n\nWhen I finally did break down and mention it to a couple of people recently, they told me they barely see anything at all. So... maybe it isn't actually there the way I see it, or maybe it's just really minor. But even knowing they don't see it, I still can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself trying to stop thinking about the uneven part of your face, but you can't?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't think I've actually tried to stop thinking about it. Like, I don't sit there and try to force it out of my head. It just sort of... happens when I'm looking at the photos from work or getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say I try to stop and can't, I just kind of focus on it when it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that thoughts about the uneven part of your face intrude into your mind when you're not intentionally thinking about it?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It doesn't just pop up out of nowhere. I mean... if I'm just going about my day or working on a schedule for an event, I'm not really thinking about it. It's just tied to when I'm actually looking at my face.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your view of the uneven part of your face is based on how it actually looks, or is it possible that your concern is not entirely based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said a few minutes ago... since the people I asked told me they barely see anything, I guess it is possible that it's not entirely based in reality. Or at least, maybe it's not as bad as I think it is. \n\nBut when I'm looking right at a photo of myself from an event, or looking in the mirror, it feels completely real to me. It looks so obvious. So, I don't know for sure... I guess my mind could just be making it a bigger deal than it actually is, even if it feels real to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just tied to when I'm actually looking at my face.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your view of the uneven part of your face is based on how it actually looks, or is it possible that your concern is not entirely based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said a few minutes ago... since the people I asked told me they barely see anything, I guess it is possible that it's not entirely based in reality. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I came in because... lately at work, I've had to be a lot more out in front of people. I plan events, so I end up in photos a lot more now. And ever since that started, I just can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in the pictures. It's really bothering me, even though everyone else tells me they barely see anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Is it only your appearance that's bothering you about this uneven-looking part of your face, or are you also worried about other things, like its potential impact on your health?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just my appearance. I'm not worried about my health or anything like that. I know it's not a medical problem... it's strictly just how uneven it looks to me, especially when I have to look at those photos.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven-looking part of your face been affecting your daily life or work in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has. Um, I recently missed part of a planning deadline because I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. It just kind of took over. And now... well, even just regular things like vendor calls and meeting prep take me a lot longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Is this concern about the uneven part of your face something that you think about all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of whether it's there every single minute or if it comes and goes. I just know it's on my mind enough to slow me down at work, like I said, but... yeah, I couldn't really tell you the exact pattern of it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about this one uneven-looking part of your face, or do you also worry about other aspects of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just this one thing. I don't really worry about anything else when it comes to how I look. It's just that one uneven part of my face that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about the uneven part of your face is causing you?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I don't know if I'd say I'm in distress, exactly. I mean, it's frustrating and it definitely gets in the way of my work, like I mentioned with the event materials. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of how much distress it causes. It just... bothers me a lot when I have to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would see this uneven part of your face as a significant problem if they looked closely at your photos?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... no. Whenever I bring it up, everyone else says they barely see anything at all. But I just can't stop staring at it in photos. That's actually why I came in to see you. Ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just fixate on this one uneven-looking part of my face, even if other people tell me it's not a big deal to them.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven part of your face caused you to avoid social interactions or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it doesn't stop me from hanging out with my friends or going out on the weekends. It's really just when I have to look at myself in pictures or videos for work that it becomes an issue for me. Outside of that, I don't really avoid social things.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend more than an hour a day thinking about the uneven part of your face?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think it's that much. I've never really timed it or anything, but... it's mostly just when I'm working with photos or videos for an event. I might get stuck looking at it then, but I wouldn't say it adds up to a whole hour every day. Once I step away from the pictures, I don't really sit around thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about the uneven part of your face happen to you every day?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not every day. Like I said, it really only comes up when I have to review media for my job. If I'm just going about a normal day where I don't have to look at pictures of myself, I don't even really think about it. It's very tied to when I actually see myself on screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of the uneven part of your face is accurate, even when others tell you they don't see it as a problem?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I don't think I've ever pointed it out to anyone else, so no one has actually told me they don't see it. I just know what I see when I'm sorting through the event photos. I guess I don't really know if my perception is completely accurate or not... it's just what stands out to me when I look at the screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the uneven part of your face is actually uneven, regardless of what others say?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do think it's actually uneven. Like I said, I haven't really asked anyone else what they think, but when I look at the photos or the videos from an event, I can see it. So... yeah, I believe what I'm seeing is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face might be exaggerated or not entirely accurate, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm not totally sure. I mean, I see it when I look at the pictures, but I don't really know if I'm making it a bigger deal in my head than it actually is. It's hard to say if it's exaggerated or not since I haven't really talked to anyone else about it. I just know it bothers me when I'm looking at the event media.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the unevenness you see in your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I guess there is a possibility. I mean, I know I said a minute ago that I hadn't really asked anyone, but... the whole reason I came in to see you is because ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just can’t stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in all the event photos. \n\nWhen I finally did break down and mention it to a couple of people recently, they told me they barely see anything at all. So... maybe it isn't actually there the way I see it, or maybe it's just really minor. But even knowing they don't see it, I still can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself trying to stop thinking about the uneven part of your face, but you can't?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't think I've actually tried to stop thinking about it. Like, I don't sit there and try to force it out of my head. It just sort of... happens when I'm looking at the photos from work or getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say I try to stop and can't, I just kind of focus on it when it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that thoughts about the uneven part of your face intrude into your mind when you're not intentionally thinking about it?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It doesn't just pop up out of nowhere. I mean... if I'm just going about my day or working on a schedule for an event, I'm not really thinking about it. It's pretty much only when I'm confronted with a picture of myself from work, or when I'm looking in the mirror getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say it intrudes when I'm doing other things. It's just tied to when I'm actually looking at my face.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your view of the uneven part of your face is based on how it actually looks, or is it possible that your concern is not entirely based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said a few minutes ago... since the people I asked told me they barely see anything, I guess it is possible that it's not entirely based in reality. Or at least, maybe it's not as bad as I think it is. \n\nBut when I'm looking right at a photo of myself from an event, or looking in the mirror, it feels completely real to me. It looks so obvious. So, I don't know for sure... I guess my mind could just be making it a bigger deal than it actually is, even if it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face could be completely wrong, even if it feels real to you?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I could say it's completely wrong. I mean... I know what I see. Even if other people tell me they don't really notice it, I don't think I'm just imagining the whole thing out of nowhere. There has to be something there for me to see it in the photos in the first place. So, maybe it's exaggerated in my head, but no, I don't think I'm completely wrong about it being there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I came in because... lately at work, I've had to be a lot more out in front of people. I plan events, so I end up in photos a lot more now. And ever since that started, I just can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in the pictures. It's really bothering me, even though everyone else tells me they barely see anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Is it only your appearance that's bothering you about this uneven-looking part of your face, or are you also worried about other things, like its potential impact on your health?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just my appearance. I'm not worried about my health or anything like that. I know it's not a medical problem... it's strictly just how uneven it looks to me, especially when I have to look at those photos.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven-looking part of your face been affecting your daily life or work in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has. Um, I recently missed part of a planning deadline because I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. It just kind of took over. And now... well, even just regular things like vendor calls and meeting prep take me a lot longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Is this concern about the uneven part of your face something that you think about all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of whether it's there every single minute or if it comes and goes. I just know it's on my mind enough to slow me down at work, like I said, but... yeah, I couldn't really tell you the exact pattern of it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about this one uneven-looking part of your face, or do you also worry about other aspects of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just this one thing. I don't really worry about anything else when it comes to how I look. It's just that one uneven part of my face that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about the uneven part of your face is causing you?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I don't know if I'd say I'm in distress, exactly. I mean, it's frustrating and it definitely gets in the way of my work, like I mentioned with the event materials. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of how much distress it causes. It just... bothers me a lot when I have to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would see this uneven part of your face as a significant problem if they looked closely at your photos?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... no. Whenever I bring it up, everyone else says they barely see anything at all. But I just can't stop staring at it in photos. That's actually why I came in to see you. Ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just fixate on this one uneven-looking part of my face, even if other people tell me it's not a big deal to them.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven part of your face caused you to avoid social interactions or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it doesn't stop me from hanging out with my friends or going out on the weekends. It's really just when I have to look at myself in pictures or videos for work that it becomes an issue for me. Outside of that, I don't really avoid social things.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend more than an hour a day thinking about the uneven part of your face?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think it's that much. I've never really timed it or anything, but... it's mostly just when I'm working with photos or videos for an event. I might get stuck looking at it then, but I wouldn't say it adds up to a whole hour every day. Once I step away from the pictures, I don't really sit around thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about the uneven part of your face happen to you every day?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not every day. Like I said, it really only comes up when I have to review media for my job. If I'm just going about a normal day where I don't have to look at pictures of myself, I don't even really think about it. It's very tied to when I actually see myself on screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of the uneven part of your face is accurate, even when others tell you they don't see it as a problem?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I don't think I've ever pointed it out to anyone else, so no one has actually told me they don't see it. I just know what I see when I'm sorting through the event photos. I guess I don't really know if my perception is completely accurate or not... it's just what stands out to me when I look at the screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the uneven part of your face is actually uneven, regardless of what others say?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do think it's actually uneven. Like I said, I haven't really asked anyone else what they think, but when I look at the photos or the videos from an event, I can see it. So... yeah, I believe what I'm seeing is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face might be exaggerated or not entirely accurate, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm not totally sure. I mean, I see it when I look at the pictures, but I don't really know if I'm making it a bigger deal in my head than it actually is. It's hard to say if it's exaggerated or not since I haven't really talked to anyone else about it. I just know it bothers me when I'm looking at the event media.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the unevenness you see in your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I guess there is a possibility. I mean, I know I said a minute ago that I hadn't really asked anyone, but... the whole reason I came in to see you is because ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just can’t stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in all the event photos. \n\nWhen I finally did break down and mention it to a couple of people recently, they told me they barely see anything at all. So... maybe it isn't actually there the way I see it, or maybe it's just really minor. But even knowing they don't see it, I still can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself trying to stop thinking about the uneven part of your face, but you can't?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't think I've actually tried to stop thinking about it. Like, I don't sit there and try to force it out of my head. So, no, I wouldn't say I try to stop and can't, I just kind of focus on it when it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that thoughts about the uneven part of your face intrude into your mind when you're not intentionally thinking about it?\n\nPatient: No, not really. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean... I've never actually been to a plastic surgeon, so I don't know exactly how I'd react. But... yeah, I think I probably would still see it.

I plan events, so I end up in photos a lot more now. And ever since that started, I just can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in the pictures. It's really bothering me, even though everyone else tells me they barely see anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Is it only your appearance that's bothering you about this uneven-looking part of your face, or are you also worried about other things, like its potential impact on your health?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just my appearance. I'm not worried about my health or anything like that. I know it's not a medical problem... it's strictly just how uneven it looks to me, especially when I have to look at those photos.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven-looking part of your face been affecting your daily life or work in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has. Um, I recently missed part of a planning deadline because I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. It just kind of took over. And now... well, even just regular things like vendor calls and meeting prep take me a lot longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Is this concern about the uneven part of your face something that you think about all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of whether it's there every single minute or if it comes and goes. I just know it's on my mind enough to slow me down at work, like I said, but... yeah, I couldn't really tell you the exact pattern of it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about this one uneven-looking part of your face, or do you also worry about other aspects of your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's really just this one thing. I don't really worry about anything else when it comes to how I look. It's just that one uneven part of my face that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about the uneven part of your face is causing you?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I don't know if I'd say I'm in distress, exactly. I mean, it's frustrating and it definitely gets in the way of my work, like I mentioned with the event materials. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of how much distress it causes. It just... bothers me a lot when I have to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would see this uneven part of your face as a significant problem if they looked closely at your photos?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... no. Whenever I bring it up, everyone else says they barely see anything at all. But I just can't stop staring at it in photos. That's actually why I came in to see you. Ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just fixate on this one uneven-looking part of my face, even if other people tell me it's not a big deal to them.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the uneven part of your face caused you to avoid social interactions or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it doesn't stop me from hanging out with my friends or going out on the weekends. It's really just when I have to look at myself in pictures or videos for work that it becomes an issue for me. Outside of that, I don't really avoid social things.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend more than an hour a day thinking about the uneven part of your face?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think it's that much. I've never really timed it or anything, but... it's mostly just when I'm working with photos or videos for an event. I might get stuck looking at it then, but I wouldn't say it adds up to a whole hour every day. Once I step away from the pictures, I don't really sit around thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about the uneven part of your face happen to you every day?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not every day. Like I said, it really only comes up when I have to review media for my job. If I'm just going about a normal day where I don't have to look at pictures of myself, I don't even really think about it. It's very tied to when I actually see myself on screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of the uneven part of your face is accurate, even when others tell you they don't see it as a problem?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I don't think I've ever pointed it out to anyone else, so no one has actually told me they don't see it. I just know what I see when I'm sorting through the event photos. I guess I don't really know if my perception is completely accurate or not... it's just what stands out to me when I look at the screen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the uneven part of your face is actually uneven, regardless of what others say?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do think it's actually uneven. Like I said, I haven't really asked anyone else what they think, but when I look at the photos or the videos from an event, I can see it. So... yeah, I believe what I'm seeing is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face might be exaggerated or not entirely accurate, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm not totally sure. I mean, I see it when I look at the pictures, but I don't really know if I'm making it a bigger deal in my head than it actually is. It's hard to say if it's exaggerated or not since I haven't really talked to anyone else about it. I just know it bothers me when I'm looking at the event media.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the unevenness you see in your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I guess there is a possibility. I mean, I know I said a minute ago that I hadn't really asked anyone, but... the whole reason I came in to see you is because ever since I’ve had to be more in front of people at work, I just can’t stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in all the event photos. \n\nWhen I finally did break down and mention it to a couple of people recently, they told me they barely see anything at all. So... maybe it isn't actually there the way I see it, or maybe it's just really minor. But even knowing they don't see it, I still can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself trying to stop thinking about the uneven part of your face, but you can't?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't think I've actually tried to stop thinking about it. Like, I don't sit there and try to force it out of my head. It just sort of... happens when I'm looking at the photos from work or getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say I try to stop and can't, I just kind of focus on it when it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that thoughts about the uneven part of your face intrude into your mind when you're not intentionally thinking about it?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It doesn't just pop up out of nowhere. I mean... if I'm just going about my day or working on a schedule for an event, I'm not really thinking about it. It's pretty much only when I'm confronted with a picture of myself from work, or when I'm looking in the mirror getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say it intrudes when I'm doing other things. It's just tied to when I'm actually looking at my face.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your view of the uneven part of your face is based on how it actually looks, or is it possible that your concern is not entirely based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said a few minutes ago... since the people I asked told me they barely see anything, I guess it is possible that it's not entirely based in reality. I guess my mind could just be making it a bigger deal than it actually is, even if it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face could be completely wrong, even if it feels real to you?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I could say it's completely wrong. But even knowing they don't see it, I still can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself trying to stop thinking about the uneven part of your face, but you can't?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't think I've actually tried to stop thinking about it. Like, I don't sit there and try to force it out of my head. It just sort of... happens when I'm looking at the photos from work or getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say I try to stop and can't, I just kind of focus on it when it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that thoughts about the uneven part of your face intrude into your mind when you're not intentionally thinking about it?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It doesn't just pop up out of nowhere. I mean... if I'm just going about my day or working on a schedule for an event, I'm not really thinking about it. It's pretty much only when I'm confronted with a picture of myself from work, or when I'm looking in the mirror getting ready. So, no, I wouldn't say it intrudes when I'm doing other things. It's just tied to when I'm actually looking at my face.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your view of the uneven part of your face is based on how it actually looks, or is it possible that your concern is not entirely based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said a few minutes ago... since the people I asked told me they barely see anything, I guess it is possible that it's not entirely based in reality. Or at least, maybe it's not as bad as I think it is. \n\nBut when I'm looking right at a photo of myself from an event, or looking in the mirror, it feels completely real to me. It looks so obvious. So, I don't know for sure... I guess my mind could just be making it a bigger deal than it actually is, even if it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your view of the uneven part of your face could be completely wrong, even if it feels real to you?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I could say it's completely wrong. I mean... I know what I see. Even if other people tell me they don't really notice it, I don't think I'm just imagining the whole thing out of nowhere. There has to be something there for me to see it in the photos in the first place. So, maybe it's exaggerated in my head, but no, I don't think I'm completely wrong about it being there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness you see in your face is not there at all, even if it looks that way to you?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think that's possible. Like I said, I'm not just making it up completely. I know people say they don't see it, and maybe I am making a bigger deal out of it than I should, but... I mean, I see it. There's definitely something there, even if it's small. It's not just totally in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see the unevenness in your face even if a doctor or a plastic surgeon told you that there is nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I mean... I've never actually been to a plastic surgeon, so I don't know exactly how I'd react. But... yeah, I think I probably would still see it. \n\nLike I was saying before, when I look in the mirror, my eyes just see what they see. Even if a doctor told me my face was perfectly fine and there was nothing to fix... I might try to believe them, or try to tell myself they're the expert, but I'm pretty sure I would still physically see the unevenness when I look at myself."
